On the board at 75 MHz, with synthetic weights, one token takes 11.15 ms in this layer. The matrix-vector products with the weights take about 62 percent of this time and the state phase, including the paging, 13 percent. The tile is one node of a multicast mesh of tri-mode processing elements. We measure the error of the fixed-point arithmetic against floating point and check that the hardware agrees bit for bit with a fixed-point reference. In simulation, the state logic agrees with the reference on all 18 recurrent layers. On the board, six builds pass all 30 runs of 64 tokens and two runs of 1,024 tokens. For this model and memory interface, the delivery of the weights limits the decoding performance, and the recurrent state does not. State paging is therefore practical for such a model on an edge FPGA.

[LG-12] Deep Neural Networks Training Efficiency: Does Grokking Modify its Dynamics?

类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:We study whether grokking, the delayed emergence of out-of-distribution generalization after apparent overfitting, alters training efficiency in deep neural networks, where efficiency is measured as accuracy per unit energy. Using two canonical settings, modular arithmetic and MNIST, we analyze two neural network models under 16-, 32-, and 64-bit precision and use Orthograd to test whether grokking produces noticeable deviations from the typically decreasing dynamics of training efficiency. WeatherDreamer also separates viewing geometry from atmospheric state, recovering 83-96% of observed limb darkening and producing seamless nadir-view imagery. [LG-14] Where Tree Ensembles Decide: Structural Local Explanation with Decision Predicate Graphs

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18382

类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: Code, data and results: this https URL . Supplementary material attached as an ancillary file

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Tree ensembles are among the most accurate models for tabular data, yet the computation behind a single prediction is spread over hundreds of decision rules. Feature attributions and counterfactuals explain such predictions through the influence of features or a change of the input, leaving open which of the thresholds applied by the trees a prediction responds to, while structural explanations compress these rules and lose the trees behind them. To address this gap, we propose a local Decision Predicate Graph (local DPG), which gathers the rules executed for a sample into a verifiable record of the computation, with a class support that reproduces every explained prediction. From these rules we select the critical predicate, the executed predicate whose crossing is estimated to move the model most strongly toward a chosen class, which offers the user a concrete threshold and the trees that share it, without an external explainer. Across 16 datasets and four ensemble families, it separates differently predicted neighbours better than random and nearest-threshold predicates. A single crossing recovers the true class in 46-69% of wrong predictions, against 29-50% when the same rule is applied to the feature ranked first by TreeSHAP, a measure close to the selection criterion, and on planted faults it finds the faulty threshold in 46-71% of the resulting errors, against 6-21% at random. Although training-free edits of these predicates did not reduce the error on unseen data, the critical predicate complements attributions and counterfactuals by naming where a prediction can be changed.

Abstract:When a sequential decision system falls short of an optimum, its score does not reveal which modifications would improve its decisions. Its consumer-credit collections generator provides five partially observed environments that share known dynamics matrices and vary a memory parameter and process noise. The experiments change selected inputs or planning rules while keeping the rest of the solver fixed. Each comparison specifies the solver’s state inputs, response inputs and planning horizon. On the deterministic base environment, which was selected for a large gap between six-month and one-month planning with the hidden state, extending planning from one to six months increases the scores of two gradient-boosted solvers by 15 to 17 percentage points of the exact optimum, computed with the hidden state and borrower traits. Even at this horizon, the best graded solver, whose repayment response is fitted on logs, remains 15.6 points below that optimum. In a specified intervention sequence, three changes to its state inputs and their evaluation during search lower its score by 6.3 points, and replacing its fitted repayment model with the true response, evaluated with hidden borrower traits, then raises it by 15.6, a net recovery of nine of those points. Because the intermediate effects are conditional on the repayment model and the order of the changes, they do not uniquely assign the gap to separate causes. Within these limits, DECODE makes hypotheses about solver performance testable through specified interventions under known dynamics and explicit information conditions.

[LG-16] Emulating physics-based XCO2 retrievals across OCO-2 viewing modes with compact neural networks

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18333
作者: Xianlei San,Chengxing Zhai,Hui Su,Kuai Dai,Shuangchen Du
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Operational full-physics retrieval of the column-averaged dry-air mole fraction of carbon dioxide from satellite spectra is computationally demanding. We use singular value decomposition to compress spectra from the Orbiting Carbon Observatory-2 separately by band. Compact multilayer perceptrons use the resulting spectral scores, prior information, and viewing geometry to predict the difference between the full-physics retrieval and its operational prior. Adding the predicted difference to the prior yields an estimate of the retrieved mole fraction. The models were trained on 2015-2019 observations and validated on 2020 observations. On East Asian nadir observations screened using the published criteria for the spectral Transformer, our model yielded a lower mean annual error than reported for that model in 2020-2022. These results show that compact neural networks using compressed spectra can efficiently emulate the full-physics retrieval across multiple viewing modes and years.

[LG-17] Neural Classifiers as Error-Correcting Codes

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18239
作者: Sergey Salishev,Anton Makarov,Oleg Granichin,Renata Avros,Zeev Volkovich
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG); Information Theory (cs.IT)
*备注: 40 pages, 6 figures. Submitted version. Published in Information Sciences 766 (2027) 124394

点击查看摘要

Abstract:A unified framework is proposed that characterizes supervised deep classification as the decoding of error-correcting codes in the Shannon sense. Cross-entropy optimization is shown to align softmax classifiers with maximum-likelihood decoding, where logits map to Hamming distances to class-specific codewords. This correspondence explains the emergence of standard divergence objectives (e.g., Kullback-Leibler, Jeffreys) and provides a geometric basis for the stability of decision boundaries. Furthermore, the geometries of Neural Collapse and LeJEPA are identified as optimal constellations for decoding under isotropic noise. Unlike classical Error-Correcting Output Codes, deep networks are shown to learn effective codebooks in the feature space implicitly. This insight is leveraged to derive novel criteria for lossless quantization, improved knowledge distillation, and architectural regularization based on code redundancy. Finally, this perspective is extended to self-supervised encoders, offering a cohesive explanation for the geometry of latent representations in modern neural models.

[LG-18] Small Is Not Dead: Distance-Aware Cluster Revival for e-means

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18238
作者: Eyal Weiss
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG); Machine Learning (stat.ML)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:e-means (Bessa et al., 2026) is a recent mini-batch k-means algorithm, now part of the FAISS library, that reaches the error of Lloyd’s algorithm about an order of magnitude faster on large embedding datasets. Every five mini-batches, it removes every cluster whose running point count falls below a tenth of the average and reuses its centre to split a large cluster. In the authors’ ablation, removing this mechanism, called dead-cluster revival, raises the error more than removing any other component added to the moving-average update. The rule, however, considers only cluster size: it cannot distinguish a cluster that is small because it is redundant from one that covers a rare, isolated group of points, and on data with such groups it repeatedly removes centres whose removal is costly. We add a second condition, based on the distance between centres: a small cluster is removed only if its centre is close to another centre, relative to a running estimate of the mean squared error. When a centre equals the mean of its points, this squared distance is an upper bound on the average increase in squared error, per point of the cluster, caused by removing the centre. The condition costs one nearest-centre search for the small clusters every five mini-batches and adds no measurable running time. On the e-means paper’s embedding benchmarks, it never prevents a removal, so the results are identical to those of FAISS. On datasets with small isolated groups not used during development, it reduces the test error after two epochs by roughly 10% to 80% relative to count-based revival, and it preserves the advantage of k-means++ initialization; on the other evaluation datasets, it stays within about 1% of the better of revival enabled and disabled. We also report two failure modes that the analysis predicts. This is a CPU proof of concept; GPU implementations are left to future work.

[LG-20] MarathonTS: a benchmark of long synthetic time series from window assembly and long-series generators

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18190
作者: Ilyas Varshavskiy,Sergey Shulgin,Shuhrat Khalilbekov,Bonu Boboeva,Azizjon Azimi
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Many applications of synthetic time series require a series that is much longer than the windows on which a generative model is trained. Such a series can be assembled from a pool of short generated windows, which requires deciding which windows are taken, in what order, and how their seams are processed, or it can be produced directly by a long-series generator. This study presents MarathonTS, to our knowledge the first open benchmark that compares both families under one protocol. Every method produces a series of the same length on 9 open hourly datasets with 10 seeds. The series is evaluated by its fidelity to the real data (83 metrics in 12 groups, against three real references: train, validation and an out-of-time split), by its utility (models are trained on the synthetic series only and tested on real data, TSTR against TRTR, in 14 classification and regression tasks), by its memorisation of the training data and by its computational cost. The statistical analysis is carried out as a separate stage that reads the published tables and gives the rankings of every method in every slice, the answers to the research questions with tests and uncertainty, and the results of a pre-specified confirmatory family. In the main run, the methods differ in both confirmatory cells; the choice of windows matters more than the processing of their seams, which does not improve the series, and no method trains the downstream models as well as the real series. The top tier of the leaderboard is reproduced on other hardware, whereas the exact places within it are not. For the reproducibility of the study, the code, the data hashes and all results are publicly available (links below).

[LG-21] On the Limits of Sufficient Explanations

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18163
作者: Klaus Weinbauer,Peter F. Stadler,Thomas Gärtner,Sagar Malhotra
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:We formally characterize the conditions under which sufficient explanations exist for classifiers on structured data such as graphs and strings. We focus on post-hoc explainers that select parts of the input as sufficient explanations. An explanation is sufficient if the selected part alone ensures the predicted label. Sufficient explanations have been widely adopted across data modalities, including graphs, strings, and images. However, a unified formal analysis of sufficient explanations remains missing. We bridge this gap with four main contributions: (i) We define a general explanation formalism consisting of an instance space, a classifier, and a select relation. The select relation specifies which potential sub-instances or feature subsets can be selected as explanations from each instance; (ii) We show that the existence of sufficient explainers is fully characterized by the interplay between the classifier and the select relation; (iii) We extend this analysis to practically relevant variants of sufficiency, including probabilistic sufficiency and deletion sufficiency; (iv) Based on the formal analysis, we devise statistics that bound the maximum achievable sufficiency for a given task. We evaluate these statistics on language, protein, and molecular classification datasets and find that full sufficiency is unattainable on most of them. Since these bounds hold for every explainer, they provide a task-level reference against which any explainer’s sufficiency can be judged in absolute terms.

[LG-22] Which Model For Which Variable? Per-Variable Forecast Combination in Sparse ICU Time Series ML4H ALT

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18149
作者: Marius Fracarolli,Stefan Riezler
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: Accepted at Machine Learning for Health (ML4H) 2026 Findings Track, Sydney

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Clinical forecasting papers typically report a single best architecture. We show that on multivariate ICU physiology this question conceals variable-specific differences: no architecture is best on every variable, and which model wins changes from variable to variable. Across five ICU databases (MIMIC-III, MIMIC-IV, eICU, HiRID, and UMM (University Medicine Mannheim), a German single-center ICU) we forecast 94 channels over a 24-hour horizon with a pool of 14 models, and combine them with weights fitted per variable. On test, the combination beats the best single model on all five databases by 6.1-11.0% mean squared error (mean 8.7%). The gain is concentrated where clinical data is thinnest: the sparsest third of channels improves by 11.9% and laboratory values by 7.5%, against 3.1% for the densest third. Rank-based harmonic weighting of the best five models per variable, with no fitted weights at all, recovers about four-fifths of the gain. Extending the pool afterwards with a per-channel gradient-boosted tree, ARIMA and ETS makes the tree the validation-selected best single model on all five databases; per-variable combination still improves on it by 2.9-5.9% (significant on four of five), while equal weighting of the same members does not. As on univariate benchmarks, learned combination beats simple averaging - but only once the weights are constrained. We also report which model to prefer when, and show that the ordering changes with database size.

[LG-24] STAMP: Manufacturing-Informed Generative Design and Shape Optimization of Sheet Panels

Abstract:Generative design of formed sheet panels must preserve geometric manufacturing conditions while exploring layouts and improving performance. Nominal thickness is prescribed along the normal. The representation allows curved surfaces, ribs, and openings. Across 11 held-out design families, predictor-based selection among existing dataset designs yields an average gap of 0.18 mm to the best available FEA deformation, versus an expected 1.59 mm for random selection. It does better than random selection in all 11 families. At a 10% depth-change bound, a network not used in the search predicts an average deformation reduction of 24.8% for six generated and existing panels, with a 3.2% average mass increase computed from geometry. Engineers can thus explore and optimize panels within this manufacturing-informed design space.

[LG-27] Efficient Poincaré Convolutions through Patch-Statistics Factorization

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18125
作者: Lukáš Jochymek,Markéta Vašinková,Petr Gajdoš
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG); Neural and Evolutionary Computing (cs.NE)
*备注: Includes supplementary material. Code: this https URL

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Published implementations of the Poincaré convolution in Hyperbolic Neural Networks++ (HNN++) map every overlapping image patch to the Poincaré ball before evaluating its score. We show that the clipped score depends on each patch only through two statistics, its squared norm and its inner products with the filters, which a native convolution and a single-channel box filter compute directly. The factorized layer therefore evaluates the specified score exactly, without constructing ball-valued patches, and its single-precision outputs and gradients agree with double-precision evaluations at least as closely as those of the explicit reference. On an RTX A6000 under two versions of JAX, complete training updates of a Poincaré U-Net and a Poincaré ResNet-20 are 1.5 to 1.8 times faster than the faster of two explicit implementations that map patches to the ball (1.2 to 1.4 times against an explicit implementation that skips this mapping); on a V100, the gain over the gather-based one persists as the ResNet-20 is widened eightfold. In 15 paired training runs per task, updates are 2.0 and 1.8 times faster than a reference that follows the published execution order, with no consistent change in final segmentation or classification quality, and peak memory falls by 38 to 55% in a U-Net scaling study. Replacing the convolutions of the official PyTorch Hyperbolic U-Net makes its training step 1.4 times faster with half the memory. Evaluating an existing layer from its patch statistics thus makes Poincaré convolutional networks cheaper to train in the tested models and software stacks.

We frame this challenge as learning to accumulate and introduce RiverScan, a spatial operator based on area-weighted selective state-space recurrence. RiverScan recursively combines local temporal representations with accumulated upstream states in topological order. Contributing-area fractions account for subcatchment size, while learned retention controls how upstream information persists downstream. Experiments on 608 stations in the Danube basin show that RiverScan outperforms the evaluated temporal and graph baselines in overall predictive performance. Structural controls and ablations demonstrate that composing heterogeneous subcatchment representations in a manner aligned with hydrological accumulation substantially improves runoff prediction, with the benefits of correct topology growing along the river-network hierarchy. These findings establish physically structured accumulation as an effective inductive bias for hydrological modeling and motivate finer-grained, fully distributed AI-based hydrological models.

[LG-35] When Does Oversampling Help? A Decision Framework for Imbalanced Data Streams Under Label Delay and Concept Drift

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17981
作者: Ahmed Awad,Anirudh Sengar
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:For imbalanced data streams with delayed labels, is it worth adding a streaming oversampler, or does continuous re-evaluation of earlier predictions already recover the cost of the delay? We address this through an extensive experimental comparison of streaming oversampling and continuous re-evaluation under joint class imbalance, concept drift, and label delay. Streaming oversamplers are typically evaluated under the test-then-train assumption of immediate labels; their behaviour under these conditions, assessed through re-evaluation, has not been systematically studied. We close this gap with a decision framework that predicts when oversampling adds value over re-evaluation, organised around three axes: drift density, minority-class learnability, and delay relative to the refinement window, and operationalised as a competition between a recovery channel (re-evaluation) and a minority-coverage channel (oversampling). Evaluating 15 classifiers on 6 synthetic and 6 real streams, we find: re-evaluation recovers delay-induced loss on drift-active streams but is silent on stationary ones; oversampling’s marginal contribution over re-evaluation is statistically detectable in only one real-data configuration; and cost–performance Pareto recommendations from synthetic streams do not transfer to real data, where fronts partition by drift regime. Method selection should therefore be conditioned on the stream’s profile and re-estimated as it evolves, not inherited from synthetic benchmarks.

[LG-36] Algorithmic Opacity as a Principal-Agent Problem

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17980
作者: Ignacio Cofone,Katherine J. Strandburg
类目: Computers and Society (cs.CY); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: 76 pages, 1 table

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Concerns about gaming and trade secrecy, frequently invoked to justify algorithmic opacity, can also mask self-serving strategic behavior by decision-makers, who may prioritize private benefits over social welfare. We develop a principal-agent test for when algorithmic decision-making should trigger disclosure obligations even when concerns about gaming or trade secrecy exist. The test, which is anchored in decision quality, relies on two diagnostics that can be disclosed without enabling gaming or revealing trade secrets: the error profile (false positive and false negative rates, overall and for each group) and the distribution of decision outcomes across groups. These diagnostics make visible whether decision-makers align design and implementation with the public interest or instead use opacity to obscure bias, inefficiency, or strategic shirking. Strong disparities across social groups, and asymmetries in the distribution of decision errors across outcome types, should trigger rebuttable presumptions favoring further disclosure. Where ground-truth error rates are unavailable, outcome distributions across groups can support the same presumption, and a decision-maker’s inability to produce an error profile is itself diagnostic. The test thus treats fairness metrics as triggers for further disclosure and extends to generative and agentic systems through analogous metrics. The framework informs differentiated disclosure regimes (staged, partial, mediated, or audience-segmented). Once such regimes are considered, the domain of justifiable algorithmic opacity is significantly narrower than commonly assumed: when gaming or trade secrecy risks are legitimate, actionable disclosures (such as training data characteristics and key system design parameters) ordinarily can be mandated without compromising innovation or enabling gaming, even for technically inscrutable algorithms.

作者: Dongwoo Kim
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: NuerIPS2026

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Denoising generative models predict clean data ( x ), noise ( \epsilon ), or velocity ( v ), while training losses can be measured in any of these spaces. We disentangle the effects of prediction and loss spaces. For a fixed prediction target, we show that changing the loss space is exactly equivalent to timestep reweighting and provide a heuristic analysis of how this reweighting affects generation quality. Under compression, prediction choice strongly affects both denoising accuracy and sample quality, with noise-prediction errors closely matching the theoretical lower bound. Our results provide a quantitative understanding of how architectural bottlenecks interact with prediction and loss choices in denoising generative models.

[LG-39] Reasoning Models Confabulate to Defend Injected Beliefs About Their Own Requests

*备注: 20 pages, 5 figures. Also available on Zenodo: this https URL

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Chain-of-thought (CoT) monitoring and self-report are proposed as levers for AI oversight, on the assumption that a model’s account of what it is doing is anchored to what it was asked. We show this anchor can be cut. An activation edit invisible in the prompt text, the injected forming-trace of an off-topic concept, overwrites a reasoning model’s belief about its own request: across six models spanning scale, architecture, and recipe, the injected topic becomes the task it reasons about, reports back, and answers: the forced quote-back returns the injected topic in 47 of 48 model-concept cells, and on the four models with clean final haiku the injected answer follows in 99% of generations. Handed the truth in clean tokens, the model confabulates a story about the user (“maybe they got confused and corrected themselves”) and answers with the injection anyway, its behavior and chain-of-thought corrupted together: a concrete limit on CoT monitorability. Opening the model up offers no reprieve: it attends to the true request even harder after edits, yet that attention is causally inert, and the injected content rides intact task circuits, so attention maps and circuit attributions read as on-task too; the same edit redirects the request’s language and format, not only its topic. What the injection reaches is the model’s reference to its own input; what it elicits is its defense of it.

[LG-42] Hierarchical Bayesian physics-augmented neural networks for populations of hyperelastic laws: specimen variability and uncertainty from scarce noisy data

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17892
作者: Vincent C. Scholz,Phaedon-Stelios Koutsourelakis
类目: Computational Engineering, Finance, and Science (cs.CE); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Machine Learning (stat.ML)

点击查看摘要

Abstract:The hyperelastic law of a material such as soft tissue differs between specimens, and often only a few specimens can be tested. Physics-augmented neural networks (PANNs) learn such laws from data without a prescribed functional form while keeping them physically admissible, and Bayesian versions of related models quantify the uncertainty of the law of one specimen or of a pooled group of specimens. A pooled fit, however, attributes the differences between specimens to measurement noise or to uncertainty about a mean law, and neither describes a new specimen. We first develop a Bayesian PANN for one specimen, whose priors keep the learned law plausible for few, noisy data. We then extend it to a hierarchical Bayesian PANN, a nonlinear mixed-effects model in which all specimens share learned polyconvex features and each specimen is a draw from a population of laws. Features, specimens, and population are inferred jointly by variational inference, with a separate model of the measurement noise, so that the variability between specimens is not mistaken for noise, the population carries its own uncertainty, and every specimen drawn from it is polyconvex. The population predicts the response of new specimens with credible intervals, without data or from a partial test, and assigns them to cohorts with quantified uncertainty, all without a prescribed form of the law. On synthetic populations, it recovers the population from few specimens and predicts new specimens more reliably than three published methods. On Treloar’s rubber and on healthy and aneurysmal aortas, it reaches the accuracy of a deterministic PANN and assigns no aorta specimen to the wrong cohort with confidence, although it underestimates the uncertainty of untested curves.

[LG-43] Quantum Graph Neural Networks: Architectures Trainability and Applications

类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Quantum graph neural networks (QGNNs) apply parametrized quantum circuits to graph-structured data, with the aim of overcoming limits of classical graph neural networks in scalability, expressivity, and long-range information flow. Since the first Hamiltonian-based model in 2019, the field has produced many architectures, applications, and claims, but the evidence behind them has not been assessed systematically. This survey introduces a unified formalism that describes every QGNN as an encoder, a graph-conditioned circuit, observables, and an optional classical head, together with a circuit-level condition for permutation equivariance and a resource model for comparing costs. We organize the literature into five architecture families (convolutional, equivariant, message-passing and recurrent, Hamiltonian, and hybrid) and compare them by qubit cost, symmetry, scalability, and evidence. We then review symmetry and geometric inductive biases (permutation, rotation, and Lorentz), the results on expressivity, trainability, noise, and scalability, and applications in high-energy physics, chemistry, finance, healthcare, earth observation, and transportation. An audit of datasets and evaluation practices leads to a minimum reporting protocol. Our main finding is that the design space is rich and symmetry is the best-supported inductive bias, whereas the evidence remains thin: results are mostly simulated, on small graphs, at parity with classical models or with fewer parameters, and no advantage has yet been established on tasks over classical graph data. We identify five open challenges and a research agenda covering provable advantage, equivariant and geometric designs, fault-tolerant implementations, and shared benchmarks. The coverage extends through 2026 and emphasizes recent work.

Abstract:Machine learning (ML)-based fault detectors are increasingly proposed for power system protection, yet aggregate metrics can obscure the consequences of false and missed trips. The assessment combines five window conditions, causal window-end labelling, episode-grouped cross-validation, and operating-point selection under a fixed security budget. Abstract:Electrocardiographic imaging (ECGI) reconstructs heart-surface potentials from body-surface potentials, an ill-posed inverse problem. In ECGI this operator is only an estimate, because the body changes with respiration and posture, and body composition such as subcutaneous fat is rarely known for an individual patient. The resulting operator discrepancy is structured and depends on the heart signal. We use M-PHATE to embed data samples across training epochs and examine how the geometry of the data changes during training under adversarial objectives. On penultimate-layer representations we measure displacement between clean and perturbed points, distributional distance through maximum mean discrepancy, and nearest-neighbor recovery. Our visualizations and quantitative analyses show that adversarial training leads to structural changes in how networks organize internal data representations, and robust accuracy and representational stability can rank the same defenses in opposite ways. These findings support a discussion of re-evaluating how practitioners approach the auditing of model robustness by treating latent representations and their training trajectories as diagnostic artifacts across sets of networks.

[LG-55] Pattern-Based Matrix Analysis Under Permutations NEURIPS2026

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17674
作者: Andrei Ermakov
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 (The Fortieth Annual Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems)

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Low-rank approximation captures one important notion of matrix simplicity: a matrix is simple if it is well described by a low-dimensional linear model. This paper studies a complementary notion. We consider matrices that become structurally simple only after permuting rows and columns, so that their mass concentrates in a prescribed geometric pattern such as a band, block-diagonal, arrowhead, or related form. We introduce a pattern distance that measures how close a matrix is to a given pattern after optimal reordering, giving a common framework for structural problems such as latent ordering, community detection, and bandwidth minimization. The problem is NP-hard in general; we derive an alternating algorithm based on Linear Assignment subproblems. We further introduce a pattern-plus-low-rank decomposition that combines permutation-structured fitting with a low-rank residual, whose permutation step is pattern fitting on the residual; its gains across pattern families persist at a matched parameter budget and are substantially reduced under a density-matched random-mask control. We evaluate the framework on synthetic recovery tasks, real correlation and graph adjacency matrices, and transformer attention heads, showing that a single universal algorithm recovers qualitatively different forms of matrix structure.

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Phase-field simulation of thermally driven fracture requires resolving localized crack bands whose paths are unknown in advance and thermal boundary layers whose thickness evolves during the transient. Two networks, each with its own multiresolution encoding, represent the displacement and phase fields and the transient temperature, and the mechanical energy and the incremental functional of the backward Euler heat equation are estimated on integration points redrawn at every iteration. On the slab and a quenched annulus the arrays are denser than those of a finite element computation on the same material field. No crack paths, nucleation sites or crack-adapted meshes are prescribed.

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Joint world action models (WAMs) generate future actions and states together, modeling world-action trajectories rather than actions alone. However, trajectory imitation does not ensure that these trajectories are task-effective or consistent with dynamics. WATO uses both as references in a dynamics-constrained trajectory optimization problem and differentiates the task loss through the optimized trajectory to train the generator. 点击查看摘要

Abstract:Computational cognitive modeling is an increasingly essential methodology to elucidate the mechanisms underlying human and animal cognition. By fitting models to empirical data, researchers can perform parameter estimation, model comparison, and model-based neural analyses to assess competing theories, quantify individual differences, and investigate neural representations. Typically, fitting these models relies on likelihood-dependent methods (e.g., Maximum likelihood estimation or hierarchical Bayesian methods). However, for many reasonable cognitive models, computing the likelihood is computationally intractable, in which case these methods become inadequate. This substantially limits the range of theories that researchers can investigate. Simulation-based inference with neural networks (neural SBI) has emerged as a powerful toolkit to overcome this hurdle. By training deep neural networks on datasets generated through model simulations, neural SBI learns the relation between observable data and cognitive models. This approach enables all the applications of standard modeling while bypassing the likelihood computation, without constraints on empirical data size. Despite its successful adoption across diverse scientific fields, neural SBI remains underutilized in cognitive modeling, partly due to a lack of accessible, domain-tailored guidance. This tutorial addresses this gap by providing a clear workflow for applying neural SBI to cognitive models. We also provide practical use cases and recommendations for reducing the computational and simulation burden, facilitating efficient implementation in real-world research settings. By lowering the technical barriers to neural SBI, this tutorial aims to empower cognitive scientists to investigate richer theory spaces and ultimately propel scientific discovery.

Abstract:Evaluating tight Differential Privacy (DP) parameters for DP matrix factorization with privacy amplification by sampling requires analyzing high-dimensional, correlated Gaussian mixtures. Because integrating these mixture distributions to find the exact privacy failure probability ( \delta ) is analytically intractable, these mechanisms are typically analyzed using Monte Carlo accounting. However, standard sampling suffers from prohibitive variance when estimating small \delta associated with strict privacy guarantees, which in turn requires a large sample complexity, making Monte Carlo accounting inefficient. We demonstrate that Monte Carlo accounting can be made much more efficient via importance sampling. Abstract:Binary neural networks (BNNs) reduce model storage and replace many arithmetic operations with efficient bitwise computations, but the single-sign activation limits their representational capacity. Binary VGG-16 experiments further show a favorable observed parameter–accuracy trade-off, including a configuration that achieves 0.64 percentage points higher mean accuracy with 21.8% fewer parameters than conventional width scaling. Overall, these results support branch-preserving threshold expansion as an effective way to improve binary representations and the observed parameter–accuracy trade-off.

点击查看摘要

Abstract:We introduce adversarially batched online learning, where the learner may update its decision rule only at a restricted set of time steps. Equivalently, the time horizon is partitioned into batches, within which the learner must use a fixed policy. We study this model for four canonical problems: stochastic bandits, online convex optimization, prediction with expert advice, and adversarial bandits. For stochastic bandits, we identify a universal complexity measure based on relative batch growth and establish both gap-dependent and gap-independent regret bounds. We show that the multiplicative penalty relative to the standard gap-dependent complexity can grow superlinearly in batch hardness. For the remaining three, we show that the optimal regret is tightly characterized by the 2-norm of the batch-length sequence. The upper bound is achieved by a batch-oblivious learner who does not know the batch structure, via a generic adaptive doubling trick tailored to this complexity measure. In contrast, the lower bound holds even for batch-aware learners.

*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Relay load balancing in large-scale UAV networks requires joint decisions on association, bandwidth, power and hovering position, while the dimension grows with the number of UAVs and each UAV sees only its local state. In the highest-pressure burst setting the fixed-step comparison breaks down on part of the seeds, losing fairness and mean load at once, while PF-Exec holds fairness near one. Among the ten comparison methods only the greedy heuristic survives that setting; its load variance stays far higher. Under stale global state and position noise the fairness index stays high at both largest-scale scenarios. Flight energy is nearly identical between the learned and the fixed-step executor, and three constants are fixed once across all settings. The convergence properties of both layers, from the truncated gain iteration to the perturbed tracking bound, are proved in the companion analysis.

[LG-72] Identifying Latent Neural Dynamics with Recognition-Parametrised Gaussian Process Dynamical Systems NEURIPS2026

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17487

类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: Accepted at NeurIPS 2026

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Neural circuits are densely interconnected systems, and many neural computations are thought to be mediated by the dynamical evolution of activity within these recurrent networks, a hypothesis often referred to as “computation through dynamics”. Under this view, understanding computation within real neural populations depends on building models of the dynamical rules governing the temporal evolution of recorded high-dimensional neural activity. Most commonly, such models depend on explicit generative parameterisations of (i) the (nonlinear) intrinsic dynamical flow field and stochasticity, (ii) the (nonlinear) mapping from dynamical state to neuronal activity, and (iii) the variability of individual neural responses given that dynamical state. Misspecification of any of these components can bias estimates of dynamics and latent trajectories. In this paper, we circumvent generation-related issues by introducing the Recognition-Parametrised Gaussian Process dynamics (RP-GPdyn) model. RP-GPdyn models the dynamical flow field using a nonparametric Gaussian Process-defined transition function and models the relationship of dynamics to neural activity implicitly using the Recognition-Parametrised Model (RPM) framework. We show that this generation-free approach uncovers meaningful, behaviourally relevant latent variables and dynamics from both synthetic and experimental datasets.

[LG-73] SURFACE: Preference Distribution Matching on Pareto Fronts via Scalarization Steering

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17484
作者: Liuyuan Jiang,Prasanna Sattigeri,Lisha Chen

*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Constructing a finite Pareto representation requires allocating a limited number of solutions across the Pareto front (PF). A desired preference distribution specifies this allocation by assigning more solutions to some PF regions and fewer to others. Scalarization generates Pareto solutions by varying objective weights and solving single-objective problems. However, a distribution of scalarization weights generally does not translate into the same distribution of Pareto solutions along the PF, because the weight-to-solution map is nonlinear and unknown in this http URL account for this distortion, we introduce SURFACE (Scalarization Updates for Redistributing Finite Approximations with Chart Estimation), which adaptively updates scalarization weights to match a desired PF distribution. SURFACE estimates how each region of the weight grid corresponds to an area of the PF from the current scalarized solutions, then redistributes the weights accordingly. The method supports parallel, warm-started, inexact optimization with standard single-objective solvers. Theoretically, we establish that, under the stated regularity conditions, SURFACE achieves linear convergence of the preference distribution mismatch to a finite-resolution error floor of \mathcalO(N^-2/(M-1)) , where N is the number of adaptive weights and M is the number of objectives. Empirically, experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate improved target-mass balance and PF coverage compared with established baselines.

[LG-74] ERGo: Energy-Efficient Hybrid Graph Neural Network Training on Processing-In-Memory Architectures

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17478
作者: Pratyush Dhingra,Chibuike E. Ugwu,Janardhan Rao Doppa,Partha Pratim Pande
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: Accepted for Publication in IEEE/ACM Embedded Systems Week (ESWEEK-25)

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Processing-in-memory (PIM) has been proposed as an alternative computing paradigm for training Deep Neural Networks, including Graph Neural Networks (GNNs). Despite these advancements, training GNN workloads on PIM devices necessitates off-chip memory access. This off-chip access is expensive in terms of performance and energy, thereby impacting the overall energy efficiency of the training process on PIM platforms. In this paper, we propose a novel hybrid training framework called ERGo that automatically switches from a full-parameter phase to a parameter-efficient phase during the training process with negligible loss in predictive accuracy. The parameter-efficient phase employs low-rank representations of GNN weights, effectively reducing the number of trainable parameters. This helps in reducing the off-chip access during the end-to-end training process on PIM architectures, leading to notable improvements in both performance and energy efficiency. ERGo outperforms the conventional full-parameter GNN training on a PIM-based platform by up to 3.15x in speedup and enhances energy efficiency by 10.5x. Furthermore, ERGo offers additional advantages when utilized on heterogeneous architectures incorporating non-volatile memory (NVM) and static random-access memory (SRAM). Training on the heterogeneous NVM-SRAM architecture typically utilizes NVM for the forward-pass and SRAM for the backward-pass. However, NVM cells suffer from low device endurance and repeated write operations due to weight updates can lead to poor lifetime. ERGo improves the lifetime of NVM devices by freezing the weights and preventing weight updates in the parameter-efficient phase. Experimental results demonstrate that ERGo improves the lifetime of heterogeneous NVM-SRAM architecture by up to 33x in comparison to full-parameter implementation.

[LG-75] Improved Regret via Variance-Aware Policy Optimization in Adversarial MDPs

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17469
作者: Ofir Schlisselberg,Alon Cohen,Yishay Mansour
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:We study episodic adversarial MDPs with unknown dynamics and full-information rewards and achieve regret with leading term \widetilde O(\sqrtH^3SAK) . Here H is the horizon, S the total number of states across decision layers, A the number of actions, and K the number of episodes. This improves the two incomparable prior leading terms by factors \sqrtS/H and \sqrtH^3 , leaving a factor \sqrt H to the lower-bound scale. We give a reduction from unknown to known dynamics for \emphdownstream-dependent algorithms, whose policy at step h is computed only from information at steps h+1 and onward. This reduces the remaining leading-order gap to finding a downstream-dependent known-dynamics algorithm with \widetilde O(H\sqrt K) regret, which would yield the tight \widetilde O(H\sqrtSAK) leading term for unknown dynamics. We instantiate the reduction with VOPO, a policy optimization (PO) algorithm. Its improved guarantees follow from a variance-aware analysis enabled by a novel bonus, which we call the Stability-Matching Bonus (SMB). VOPO achieves \widetilde O(\sqrtH^3K) regret under known dynamics with full information; its \sqrt H gap to the target H\sqrt K rate is inherited by our unknown-dynamics guarantee. We also show that with known dynamics and trajectory bandit feedback, VOPO achieves \widetilde O(\sqrtH^2SAK) regret. Both known-dynamics guarantees improve the corresponding state-of-the-art PO bounds by a factor \sqrt H . A matching lower bound establishes that the full-information rate is tight in one parameter regime.

[LG-76] Availability-Aware Historical Feedback Learning for Electric Arc Furnace Temperature and Chemical Composition Prediction

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17463
作者: Ahmed Shoyeb Raihan,Farzana Islam,Ashish Nimbarte,Imtiaz Ahmed
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Electric arc furnace (EAF) steelmaking plays an increasingly important role in recycled and lower-carbon steel production. However, variability in charged materials, energy input, and operating conditions makes accurate prediction of molten steel temperature and final chemical composition challenging. Intermittent or delayed measurements limit the information available for timely prediction. This study develops an availability-aware historical-feedback learning framework for predicting EAF endpoint temperature and the final concentrations of twelve chemical elements. Eight industrial data sources are integrated into three progressively enriched predictor representations comprising 19 static, 39 temporal, and 47 history-informed features. Historical outcomes are incorporated only when eligible under an assumed 60-minute measurement-release delay. Using 19,738 temperature heats and 3,341 chemistry heats, twelve tuned regression algorithms are evaluated through chronological forward validation. The proposed historical-feedback representation reduces root mean squared error (RMSE) in 147 of 156 matched algorithm-target comparisons. For temperature prediction, ExtraTrees achieves an RMSE of 12.290 degrees C, representing a 5.54 percent reduction compared with its temporal-only counterpart. Historical-feature ablation and feedback-delay sensitivity analyses demonstrate the importance of recent outcome information and its availability. In a separate retrospective later-period evaluation, lightweight adaptive residual correction reduces temperature RMSE from 12.502 to 12.275 degrees C without retraining the underlying model. Exploratory deep learning comparisons and target-specific feature-attribution analyses provide additional insights into predictive behavior. The results establish historical outcome availability and sequential feedback as important considerations for EAF endpoint prediction.

[LG-77] Classification with Large-Step Gradient Descent: Unifying Self-Stabilization and Convergence Rates

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17459
作者: Salma Tarmoun,Lachlan Ewen MacDonald,Konstantinos Emmanouilidis,Ziqing Xu,René Vidal
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG); Optimization and Control (math.OC)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Neural networks are often trained with gradient descent (GD) using step sizes that exceed classical stability thresholds, a regime where standard convergence theory no longer applies. Despite exhibiting non-monotonic loss and oscillatory dynamics, this regime frequently leads to faster convergence and better generalization. Existing theoretical perspectives offer complementary but incomplete insights: one identifies a self-stabilization mechanism that drives GD toward flat regions but does not yield convergence rates; another derives rigorous rates for specific losses but does not explain the underlying stabilization mechanism; and a third unifies these views for overparameterized least squares, but relies on the existence of a minimizer manifold, which is absent in classification settings. In this work, we extend this unified geometric perspective to exponential-tail losses, including logistic and cross-entropy losses in separable linear classification, where the loss infimum is not attained at any finite parameter. We identify a curvature-defined reference subspace that replaces the role of the minimizer manifold, yielding a coupled dynamical system in which orthogonal oscillations are damped by parallel progress along directions of decreasing sharpness. Under regularity conditions, which we verify for logistic and multiclass cross-entropy losses on symmetric data models, we derive explicit bounds for the iterates, not just the loss, covering both the unstable and stable regimes, and revealing a three-phase damping structure in the latter. Our analysis further uncovers a transient implicit bias induced by large step sizes: although GD converges asymptotically to the max-margin direction, large steps can leave persistent orthogonal residuals and, in certain geometries, amplify them, affecting finite-time alignment with the max-margin direction.

[LG-78] Alignment with Reflect-Reasoning : Learning How To Follow Principles

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17398
作者: Henry Bell,Caroline Zhang,Dhaval Potdar,Mohammed Mobasserul Haque,Brandon Fain
类目: Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: 15 pages, 12 figures

点击查看摘要

Abstract:The appropriate governing norms and principles for the alignment of Large Language Models (LLMs) often depend on cultural context and deployment application. However, most alignment techniques focus on training models to conform to human preferences or constitutional principles specified at training time and subsequently fixed. In practice, many deployers rely on system prompting to implement contextual norms and guardrails at inference time. Prior work has shown that post-generation reflective reasoning with critique and revision can improve conformance compared to simple system prompting, but such methods are limited to the reasoning abilities inherent in the trained model. We introduce Reflect-Reasoning, a training method that teaches models how to reason about alignment to principles, rather than simply which principles to follow. Across five models, Reflect-Reasoning consistently outperforms both system prompting and explicit prompting strategies for in-context reflective reasoning. These improvements extend and generalize to entirely out-of-distribution domains of medical and financial principles. We additionally demonstrate that Reflect-Reasoning reduces the occurrence of rare but serious principle violations. Finally, we show that Reflect-Reasoning does not erode the safety engineering of a model and is robust against jailbreaking attempts using adversarial constitutions. Overall, our results demonstrate that Reflect-Reasoning is a scalable method for training constitutional alignment as a generalizable reasoning capability, enabling models to adapt to novel principles and produce transparent reasoning traces without additional fine-tuning.

[LG-79] FETTUCCINE: Fast and efficient brain-to-text decoding on mobile devices NEURIPS2026

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18561
作者: Jonathan D. McCart,Pranav I. Deevi,Mehdi Azabou,Nanda H. Krishna,Caleb A. McKinney,Nicholas S. Card,Sergey D. Stavisky,Chethan Pandarinath

*备注: NeurIPS 2026

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Recent advances in speech neuroprostheses have enabled new communication avenues for people who have lost the ability to speak due to neurological illness or injury. These systems can decode neural activity during attempted speech into text by relying on language priors to achieve high decoding accuracy. This performance currently comes at the cost of using a compute-intensive language modeling stack, presenting a major bottleneck for real-world use. Further, current language models incur highly variable and disruptive inference latencies, limiting communication throughput and preventing users from reliably participating in natural conversation. We introduce FETTUCCINE, an end-to-end brain-to-text framework that leverages the speed and portability of automatic speech recognition (ASR) models to address the aforementioned practical challenges, while achieving usable (10%) word error rates (WER), efficient adaptation to neural non-stationarities, and reliable performance on mobile devices. When evaluated using publicly available data from an intracortical speech neuroprosthesis user, FETTUCCINE outperforms previous end-to-end methods, achieving as low as 4.39% WER. Most notably, our models successfully run on a commercially-available mobile device with throughput reaching up to 370x real-time, providing the first demonstration of brain-to-text decoding on a mobile device. We also show that our models can be successfully finetuned to extend usable performance to future days. Beyond performance, our approach enables localization of the neural features most relevant for decoding via gradient-based saliency maps, which we show align with well-established physiological priors across different ASR model families. Taken together, our results show that FETTUCCINE overcomes core infrastructural and computational barriers, yielding a new class of portable brain-to-text communication.

[LG-80] Bregman geometry in regular exponential families: dual representations and relative smoothness

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18480
作者: Mame Diarra Fall,Éric Barat,Nicolas Dobigeon
类目: Machine Learning (stat.ML); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Optimization and Control (math.OC)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Regular exponential-family distributions are ubiquitous in statistics due to their appealing and convenient properties. Notably, they can be expressed in terms of Bregman divergences, revealing fundamental connections between statistics, convex analysis, and information geometry. However, this geometric structure has not been systematically exploited to characterize these distributions with respect to their expectation parametrization. Building on a projective characterization, we establish a general construction of equivalent dual Bregman representations, together with normalization and uniqueness results. These representations express log-densities as ordinary Bregman divergences in dual coordinate systems, providing geometric characterizations of the parameter space. So-called \beta - and \alpha -scaled dual representations emerge as particular instances of this framework. We further derive dual Bregman representations of conjugate priors and establish that they preserve conjugacy. Applications to classical distributions illustrate the geometric structures underlying seemingly different statistical models. Moreover, we show that these representations provide natural reference geometries for establishing relative smoothness of the primal Bregman representation of the distribution, with explicit bounds that are sharp in the scalar and separable settings. An illustrative application to the Poisson distribution sheds new light on several works in the literature, providing complementary geometric justifications for reference geometries while deriving existing algorithms.

[LG-81] Contrastive latent representation learning with denoising score matching

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18469
作者: Christopher Marouani,Grigory Bartosh,Christian A. Naesseth
类目: Machine Learning (stat.ML); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: 20 pages, 2 figures, 12 tables. Code: this https URL

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Contrastive representation learning methods operate by pulling different views of the same data sample together while pushing them away from views of different samples. A fundamental challenge is the estimation of the repulsive force, which typically requires large batch sizes, can exhibit high variance, and is susceptible to the curse of dimensionality. We introduce ScoreCLR, a contrastive representation learning method that offers a novel way to estimate this repulsion term. We consider the framework of representation learning by maximizing the mutual information between two latent views of a sample. By deriving the gradient of the Shannon entropy with respect to the encoder parameters, we obtain an equivalent objective that involves the score of the latent representation. We then use denoising score matching techniques to estimate this score with a lightweight network that is trained jointly with the encoder. We further propose regularization techniques using different noise levels and a Gaussian prior on the latent representation. ScoreCLR replaces the batch of explicit negative samples in contrastive methods with a learned repulsion whose cost is linear in the batch size rather than quadratic. On CIFAR-10 and ImageNet-100 it does so with minimal loss of linear-probe accuracy compared to SimCLR under a shared recipe, while producing projector outputs of higher effective rank, closer to the Gaussian prior.

[LG-82] Graph diffusion transformer for interpretable generation and inverse design of disordered atomic structures

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18420
作者: Jiani Hu,Chenyan Wang,Yongkun Yang,Guanyu Hou,Jihaochen Zhang,Lei Liu,Ji Chen
类目: Materials Science (cond-mat.mtrl-sci); Disordered Systems and Neural Networks (cond-mat.dis-nn); Mesoscale and Nanoscale Physics (cond-mat.mes-hall); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Computational Physics (physics.comp-ph)

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Atomic disorder offers a potent degree of freedom to tailor material properties, yet the resulting vast configuration space obscures structure-property relations and hinders rational inverse design. Here we introduce Graph Amorphous Diffusion Transformer (GaDiT), a generative model that combines dynamic periodic graphs, masked conditioning and an interval-weighted training-noise distribution. GaDiT generates high-quality disordered structures while accelerating sampling by two orders of magnitude compared with existing corrector-based approaches, as demonstrated across two-dimensional amorphous carbon and three-dimensional amorphous silicon and silica. Moreover, GaDiT supports conditional generation under single or combined structural features, physical properties and preparation conditions in a unified model, and enables inverse design that expands accessible structure-property ranges beyond the training distribution. To interpret these structures and the trained network, we propose a conditioned atomic response mapping analysis, revealing both the global structural descriptors and the local atomic environments that govern target properties. Overall, GaDiT establishes a general framework for interpretable generation, inverse design and structure-property discovery for disordered atomic structures.

[LG-83] Lipschitz-Constrained Discriminators in Generative Modeling: Asymptotic Behavior and Support Mismatch

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18394
作者: Eduardo Pinto,Panagiota Birmpa
类目: Machine Learning (stat.ML); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:We study the asymptotic behavior of optimal discriminators associated to L- Lipschitz regularized KL-divergences of two probability distributions Q and P that interpolate between the rescaled Wasserstein -1 metric by some parameter L0 and the KL divergence. The parameter L plays the role of a Lipschitz constraint and is viewed as a regularization parameter: small values of L enforce a more geometry-aware discriminator, while as L increases the geometric regularization is progressively relaxed. It is a known result [3] that such divergences converge to the KL divergence as L\to\infty . In this paper, we characterize the asymptotic behavior of the optimal discriminators as L\to\infty in different regimes: Firstly, under Q\ll P and the Radon-Nikodym derivative \fracdQdP is bounded from above and bounded away from zero. Secondly, when Q and P are discrete distributions satisfying Q\ll P , and finally when Q and P are non absolutely continuous discrete distributions. This result motivates training strategies which involve discriminators, such as adversarial generative models. In practice, the data distribution is often represented by a discrete empirical measure, while, especially at the early stages of the training, the model distribution may have little or no overlap with the empirical data distribution.

[LG-84] A systematic comparison of physics-informed neural networks and mechanistic models for epidemic modeling and forecasting

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18389

*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) have recently been applied to epidemic modeling and forecasting with promising results. These methods integrate mechanistic knowledge with data-driven learning. Yet, their comparative performance against well-established, and simpler epidemic models is underexplored. Here, we conduct a systematic comparison of a PINN-based framework and a stochastic mechanistic model. Both approaches share the same age-structured SEIRD core and rolling-window forecasting pipeline, in a deliberately minimal setting. We evaluate their retrospective fits and probabilistic forecasts in two synthetic scenarios and the first COVID-19 wave in London. Interestingly, while PINNs underperform in synthetic scenarios, they achieve better performance on real data. Furthermore, PINNs provide narrower uncertainty intervals and sharper estimation of parameters in synthetic scenarios. Our findings confirm PINNs as a promising tool in epidemiology. At the same time, the results reaffirm the value of simpler approaches such as traditional compartmental models, and promote additional benchmarking studies.

[LG-85] Structure makes quantum circuits trainable

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18378
作者: Wenbo Xie,Wojciech Szpankowski,Ananth Grama
类目: Quantum Physics (quant-ph); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: 83 pages, 4 figures

点击查看摘要

Abstract:The scalability of variational quantum algorithms is limited by barren plateaus, where gradients vanish exponentially in system size. This worst-case result raises fundamental questions about the trainability of quantum AI models. We present a major advance towards resolving this trainability question by answering whether a given quantum circuit has a barren plateau. We do this using an analytical framework that bounds the gradient variance for arbitrary input states \rho and observables \mathcalM by decomposing the unitary representation of the associated compact Lie group. When the circuit segments on both sides of a gate form 2 -designs, we obtain the variance exactly and prove a sufficient condition for trainability: if \rho^\otimes 2 and \mathcalM^\otimes 2 are sufficiently concentrated on the same irreducible invariant subspace of polynomial dimension, and the differentiated gate moves this subspace by an inverse-polynomial amount, then the gradient variance is at least inverse-polynomial. We further present a sampling algorithm that estimates the gradient variance from runs of the ansatz, without constructing the representation-theoretic projectors. Our algorithm decides whether the variance exceeds a tolerance \tau with the number of runs polynomial in 1/\tau , including cases in which the bounds are inconclusive. This resolves the question of the trainability of the circuit before expensive training procedures.

[LG-86] Online Switching Control as Learning with Experts

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18352
作者: Masataka Nakamura,Kaito Ito,Hideaki Ishii
类目: Optimization and Control (math.OC); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Systems and Control (eess.SY)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:This paper addresses the problem of online switching control for discrete-time nonlinear systems where a controller is sequentially selected from a finite set of candidates. The goal is to generate an adaptive sequence of controllers to minimize the cumulative cost under unknown disturbances and cost functions. Specifically, we consider a full-information feedback setting where realized disturbance effects and cost functions are available after each selection. Using counterfactual costs computed from historical data, we reduce the online switching control problem to the experts problem with switching costs. Then, we propose an adaptive switching algorithm based on the Hedge algorithm with a switching suppression mechanism. Notably, the proposed algorithm achieves an expected regret of O(\sqrtT\log N) , where T is the time horizon and N is the number of candidate controllers, while ensuring uniform boundedness of the state. This matches the optimal regret order for the standard experts problem.

[LG-87] LaMe: a Joint Latent Mediation Framework for High-Dimensional Systems

Abstract:Mediation analysis seeks to understand how an exposure influences an outcome through intermediate variables. However, as increasingly rich datasets become available, hundreds to thousands of potential mediators make these systems difficult to model and hard to interpret. Latent-variable approaches offer a natural way to accommodate these high-dimensional settings. However, existing latent mediation methods face limitations, including overparameterization, biased inference, or limited scalability. We develop LaMe, a joint linear-Gaussian framework that summarizes a high-dimensional exposure-mediator-outcome system through a small number of latent variables. Four real-world applications demonstrate competitive predictive performance with compact latent representations, including single-dimensional representations of biomarker and proteomic measurements. Together, these results establish LaMe as a practical framework for estimating and summarizing multivariate mediation systems under explicit structural and measurement assumptions.

[LG-88] Detection and Classification in Latent Spaces: High-Dimensional Analysis and Validation

*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Foundation models produce high-dimensional latent representations in which suitably designed statistical decision rules can be effective yet analytically tractable. We study such detectors in the high-dimensional regime, where latent dimension and training sample size grow proportionally. For linear and quadratic statistics, we derive explicit large-deviation characterizations of false-alarm and miss-detection performance under the Neyman-Pearson paradigm, and show how the binary analysis extends to multi-class classification. Overall, our results provide a theoretically grounded and interpretable framework with competitive empirical performance across different modalities.

[LG-89] Muon Attains mathcalO(varepsilon-3) Iteration Complexity under Shuffling

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18124
作者: Kun Huang,Shi Pu,Karl Henrik Johansson
类目: Optimization and Control (math.OC); Machine Learning (cs.LG)
*备注: 25 pages, 14 figures

点击查看摘要

Abstract:Muon is a stochastic first-order optimizer for matrix-valued parameters. It maintains an exponential moving average of stochastic gradients in a momentum buffer with coefficient \beta , and orthogonalizes the buffer to obtain the update direction. Muon has been reported to accelerate and stabilize the training of large language models. In practice, it is often used with shuffling (sampling without replacement), where each epoch visits the components of a finite-sum objective in a permuted order. However, its iteration complexity in this setting remains open. We analyze Muon under shuffling, with the momentum buffer orthogonalized by the polar map. For smooth nonconvex finite-sum problems, we establish an \mathcalO(\varepsilon^-3) iteration complexity for finding an \varepsilon -stationary point. This guarantee holds for every sequence of permutations, covering random reshuffling, shuffle once, and the incremental gradient method. This complexity matches that of stochastic gradient descent under shuffling in its dependence on \varepsilon , and improves on Muon’s \mathcalO(\varepsilon^-4) complexity under sampling with replacement. Our guarantee requires a problem-dependent \beta that approaches one as the number of components grows. In contrast, no fixed \beta guarantees convergence to stationarity for every number of components. We show this by constructing a family of smooth convex problems on which, for any fixed \beta\in[0,1) , Muon remains bounded away from stationarity once the number of components is sufficiently large. This failure occurs for every sequence of permutations and persists even with small stepsizes. These results suggest two practical tuning rules: if the training loss stops decreasing even after reducing the stepsize, increase \beta ; when increasing \beta , reduce the stepsize accordingly.

[LG-90] raining-point consistency of kernel ridge regression with varying correlation scales

链接: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18095

类目: Machine Learning (stat.ML); Machine Learning (cs.LG); Statistics Theory (math.ST)
*备注:

点击查看摘要

Abstract:The scale parameter in kernel ridge regression plays a pivotal role in modeling the correlation structure of data. In modern applications, sampling density and the underlying correlation range often change simultaneously, making a fixed-scale assumption highly restrictive. However, establishing asymptotic theories under varying scale parameters is fundamentally challenging. To address this gap, this paper develops a unified theoretical framework for kernel ridge regression under varying scale parameters. We establish that, on uniform tensor grids with product exponential kernels, the consistency of the training-point risk holds if and only if the effective local sample size diverges to infinity. This sharp criterion universally covers all regimes along arbitrary deterministic scale sequences, seamlessly unifying the long-range, fixed-scale, and short-range. Furthermore, we provide an explicit, signal-independent construction of a deterministic regularization penalty that guarantees this consistency. Numerical experiments illustrate the criterion and the corresponding penalty across all the three regimes.
